Let me understand the task. We have STLC with locally nameless binders, call-by-value semantics, and we need to prove strong normalization of closed well-typed terms.

Key observations:
1. Terms use locally nameless: `tm_bvar n` for bound variables, `tm_fvar x` for free variables.
2. `open_rec k u t` substitutes `u` for `bvar k`.
3. `open t u = open_rec 0 u t`.
4. `lc_at k t` means all bvars in t are < k (locally closed at level k).
5. `value` includes abs with locally_closed, true, false.
6. `step`: beta, app1 (with `locally_closed t2` side condition!), app2.
7. Typing: T_Var for fvar, T_Abs uses opening with fresh fvar, T_App, T_True, T_False.
8. Goal: forall t T, empty |-- t \in T -> strongly_normalizing t.

Another alternative: Use Coq's `Acc` and prove a *Kripke-style* logical relation where environments are part of the definition, avoiding explicit substitution lemma by treating free variables as values in the environment. This is the standard approach: define the semantic interpretation of contexts and types directly:

```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop := ...
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (rho : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (rho x).
```
Then fundamental theorem: `Gamma |- t \in T -> forall rho, env_ok Gamma rho -> LR T (subst rho t)`.

```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=

Let me think about what's needed for the T_Abs case with this LR:
Goal: `LR (T1->T2) (abs T1 t1)`, i.e., `SN (abs T1 t1) /\ forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 t1) u)`.
- `SN (abs T1 t1)`: values are SN. Need lemma `value t -> SN t`, by inversion on step showing no step from values. Actually we need "no step from value" lemma: `value t -> ~ (t --> t')`. Prove by inversion on value and step. Should be straightforward.
- Given u with LR T1 u, show LR T2 (app (abs T1 t1) u).
  - For T2 = Bool: need SN (app (abs T1 t1) u). Use SN_intro; analyze step. Reducts: (a) ST_AppAbs: open t1 u (needs value u! but u might not be a value — LR T1 u does not imply value u. Hmm, ST_AppAbs requires value v where v = u. If u not a value, no beta step.) (b) ST_App2: u --> u', app (abs) u'. (c) ST_App1: no.
    So for reducts of type (b), we need SN (app (abs T1 t1) u') — by induction on SN u? Since u is SN (does LR T1 u imply SN u? For Bool yes; for arrow yes by definition, since LR includes SN). So LR T u -> SN u always. Then use well-founded induction on `SN u` to prove: for all u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 t1) u) — hmm but this is exactly what we're proving and the induction is on u.
    
    Structure: prove a general lemma:

```coq

```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t

**L1. `LR_sn : forall T t, LR T t -> SN t`.** By destruct T; auto. Actually LR defined with match; for arrow it's the second component. Fine.

**L2. `LR_lc : forall T t, LR T t -> locally_closed t`.**

**L3. `LR_step : forall T t t', LR T t -> step t t' -> LR T t'`** (closure under one-step reduction). Proof by induction on T.
- Bool: lc of t' from lc t and step preserves lc (lemma `step_lc`), SN from SN t and step: need `SN_step : SN t -> t --> t' -> SN t'`. Prove by inversion: SN t = SN_intro t H; then H t' Hstep. 
- Arrow A B: Need lc t' (step_lc), SN t' (SN_step), and forall u, LR A u -> LR B (app t' u). Given u related: we know LR B (app t u) from LR at arrow t. We need LR B (app t' u). We have `app t u --> app t' u` by ST_App1 with lc u (from LR_lc A u). Then IH on B: LR_step B gives LR B (app t' u). Wait, IH at B says: forall t t', LR B t -> step t t' -> LR B t'. Apply with t := app t u, t' := app t' u. ✓. So induction on the type T (structural) works because the recursive call is at B which is a subterm of arrow type; for nested arrows, B is structurally smaller. For T = Bool we're done. Note the induction hypothesis for T = (A -> B) needs IH only at B (and A? A not needed except for LR A u to get lc u). Coq's `induction T` with the fixpoint LR will give IHs for T1 and T2. But the lemma is `forall t t', LR T t -> step t t' -> LR T t'`; induction on T gives IH for T1 and T2. We use IH T2. ✓.

Wait, but LR_step at arrow needs `locally_closed u` from LR A u, so uses LR_lc at A. LR_lc itself is proven by induction on T trivially. OK.

Hold on. The fundamental theorem: what exactly do we prove by induction on the typing derivation? The typing derivation is `Gamma |-- t \in T`. We want `empty |-- t \in T -> LR T t`. But the induction through T_Abs requires a generalized statement for arbitrary Gamma because the IH applies to `x |-> T1; Gamma |-- open t1 (fvar x) \in T2`. We need to define what `Gamma |-- t \in T` means semantically. Options:

(a) Environment-based: `sem_typing Gamma t T := forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t)` where sigma : atom -> tm maps free vars to terms and subst is a capture-avoiding substitution for fvars (locally nameless makes this easy? substituting fvars in a locally nameless term is a simple parallel substitution that doesn't need capture-avoidance! Because bound variables are de Bruijn; fvars are never bound. So `subst_fvar sigma t` is a straightforward fixpoint:
```
Fixpoint subst_fvar (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar n => tm_bvar n
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (...)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_fvar sigma t1)  (* no shift needed! *)
  ...
```
Yes! This is much easier than named substitution. And opening commutes with fvar-substitution in a simple way: `subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t)`? Hmm, careful: `open_rec k u t` replaces `bvar k` with u; substituting fvars doesn't touch bvars, so it commutes: yes, straightforward induction. Actually the statement `subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t)` holds definitionally for all sigma and u? RHS = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)) = tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t1)). ✓ Yes, holds syntactically without any bvar shift because open_rec's k is absolute and sigma acts only on fvars.     * lc ✓.

    * forall w, LR B1 w -> LR B2 (app (app (abs A t1) u) w). Given LR B1 w. Need LR B2 (app (app (abs) u) w). Prove by *structural induction on B2*? Hmm, B2 is a subterm of B = B1->B2, so we could have an IH at B2 from the structural induction on B. But our main induction is on SN u, with B generalized; we don't have structural induction on B available unless we do a nested induction or prove Lemma D by induction on B with an inner induction on SN u. Let's consider proving D by structural induction on B, and in each case proving the SN/forall clauses using well-founded induction on SN u. Perhaps cleaner: formulate D as:

    `D : forall u, SN u -> LR A u -> forall B t1, lc_at 1 t1 -> (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u)`
    proven by induction on B (structural), with each case using an inner induction on SN u? Hmm, the inner induction needs the statement D at the *same* B for reducts u', and at subterms of B for the forall-w clause. Let's see:
    
    For B = B1 -> B2:
      - forall w: LR B2 (app (app (abs) u) w). Hmm, but that's not of the form LR B2 (open t1 ?). So the bc premise for B2 doesn't follow directly.       Alternative: Prove LR B2 (app (app (abs) u) w) using a *different* lemma: `LR A u -> LR B1 w -> LR B2 (app (app (abs A t1) u) w)` given bc. We can prove this by the same expansion argument with SN induction on u (and maybe on w). The reducts analysis above works with IH on u'. Hmm, but the IH on u would be at type B2 with "argument" ... hmm, the term is app (app abs u) w, not of the form app abs ?.

Let me reconsider. Maybe define the logical relation differently to make the proof smoother. A common trick for CBV: define LR at function type as:

`LR (A->B) t := lc t /\ SN t /\ forall u, LR A u -> LR B (app t u)`.

The trouble is proving the forall clause for `t = app (abs) u`. An alternative: make LR at function type require t to be *SN and all its "applications to related values" are related*. What if we define LR to require for all *values* u? For CBV, well-typed terms of function type, when applied to an argument, evaluate the argument to a value first. Hmm, but LR A u means u is any strongly normalizing term of type A, which reduces to a value. The application `t u` will first reduce u. So the analysis via ST_App2 with IH on SN u handles that. The forall-w clause arises only because LR at function type is defined extensionally.

Alternative definition of LR at function type (the "hereditary" or "value-based" one):

`LR (A->B) t := lc t /\ SN t /\ (forall v, value v -> LR A v -> LR B (app t v))`

Would that be enough for the fundamental theorem? At T_App: from LR (A->B) t1 and LR A t2, we'd need LR B (app t1 t2) where t2 might not be a value. So we'd need a lemma: LR A t2 implies... we need to show LR B (app t1 t2) for non-value t2. We could prove by induction on SN t2: if t2 is a value, use the clause; if not, app t1 t2 steps via ST_App2 (if t1 value) — but t1 might not be a value either. Ugh, both may be non-values. This is the standard reason to define LR at function types with arbitrary related arguments (which the definition above does).

Let's reconsider: maybe the forall-w clause is provable by a *mutual induction* where the statement is:

`Lemma D_gen : forall B, ... forall t, lc t -> SN t -> (forall t', step t t' -> LR B t') -> LR B t` — the expansion lemma (Lemma C), plus a separate "value/abstraction introduction" lemma:
`Lemma E : forall A B t1, lc_at 1 t1 -> (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) -> forall u, LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u)`.

Let's attempt to prove E using Lemma C at B. To apply Lemma C at B we need:
- lc (app abs u) ✓
- SN (app abs u) — prove this separately with SN induction on u, call it `Lemma F`.
- forall x, step (app abs u) x -> LR B x.
  * ST_AppAbs: x = open t1 u, bc gives LR B ✓.
  * ST_App1: impossible ✓.
  * ST_App2: u --> u' (value abs), x = app abs u'. Need LR B (app abs u'). By Lemma C at B! Circular (same size u vs u' — but u' is a reduct, so SN u' smaller). Hmm, but Lemma C doesn't have an SN-u parameter. Maybe prove E by induction on SN u, and use Lemma C at B for the reduct app abs u'? Let's check Lemma C's proof dependency: C at B = B1->B2 needs, for the forall-w clause, LR B2 (app (app t u) w)... but with C's hypothesis being "all reducts of t are LR B". For t = app abs u, hmm.

Let me restructure with the following key insight: prove the expansion lemma C *and* the application lemma E *mutually by induction on types*, or more precisely prove a single strong lemma by induction on the type with a well-founded induction on the SN structure of terms.

Actually, let's look at how this is done in standard textbooks for CBV strong normalization. I recall the proof in "Types and Programming Languages" is for full TAPL with references etc. and uses a different method. For pure STLC, here's a classical approach:

Define `SN t` (strongly normalizing). Then define `R_T` as a set of terms:
- `R_Bool = {t | SN t}`
- `R_{A->B} = {t | SN t ∧ ∀u ∈ R_A. t u ∈ R_B}`

Prove:
1. If `t ∈ R_T` then `SN t`. (trivial)
2. If `t ∈ R_T` and `t --> t'` then `t' ∈ R_T` (closure under reduction). — Proof by induction on type. (We sketched: uses lc u for ST_App1. In locally nameless we need lc in R_T. In named presentations, no lc issue.)
3. If `t ∈ R_T` and `t -->* t'`? maybe.
4. Fundamental theorem.
5. For the abstraction case: need: if `∀u ∈ R_A. t u ∈ R_B` and `t = λx.M` with `∀u ∈ R_A. M[x:=u] ∈ R_B`, then `t ∈ R_{A->B}`. This requires showing `SN t` (value) and `∀u ∈ R_A. t u ∈ R_B`.

The proof of item 5's application claim: Given `∀u ∈ R_A. M[x:=u] ∈ R_B`, show `∀u ∈ R_A. (λx.M) u ∈ R_B`. Induction on the derivation of `SN u` (since u ∈ R_A implies SN u) plus induction on the type B? Let's write out:
- Base B = Bool: `(λx.M) u` steps to `M[x:=u]` ∈ R_Bool (SN), or to `(λx.M) u'` for u --> u'. By IH on SN u', u' ∈ R_A (closure under reduction) and SN u', so `(λx.M) u' ∈ R_Bool`. So all one-step reducts of `(λx.M)u` are SN, hence it's SN. ✓
- B = B1 -> B2: need SN (as above) and ∀w ∈ R_{B1}. `(λx.M) u w` ∈ R_{B2}. Now `(λx.M) u w` is `((λx.M) u) w`. Analyze its reducts:
   * The function part `(λx.M) u` steps to `M[x:=u]` or `(λx.M) u'`; via ST_App1 (needs lc w ∈ R_{B1} via lc in R), gives `M[x:=u] w` or `(λx.M) u' w`.
   * `M[x:=u] ∈ R_{B}` where B = B1->B2, so `M[x:=u] w ∈ R_{B2}` by the definition. ✓
   * For `(λx.M) u' w`: by IH on SN u' (with w ∈ R_{B1}), `(λx.M) u' w ∈ R_{B2}`. Hmm, the IH at u' must cover the "forall w" part; so the induction statement should be: for all w ∈ R_{B1}, `(λx.M) u w ∈ R_{B2}` — that's what we're proving by SN induction on u. So the statement at u': forall w ∈ R_{B1}, `(λx.M) u' w ∈ R_{B2}` ✓.
   * ST_App2 on the outer app requires `value ((λx.M) u)` — false. ST_AppAbs on the outer requires `(λx.M) u` to be an abstraction — false (it's an app).
   * So all one-step reducts of `(λx.M) u w` are in R_{B2}. Do we know SN of `(λx.M) u w`? We need it to apply an "expansion" lemma. Hmm, R_{B2} = SN ∧ forall. To get R_{B2} of `(λx.M) u w` we need SN and the forall clause. The forall clause recursively: `(λx.M) u w w'`... We can handle this by the expansion lemma C at type B2, IF C is available. But C at B2 requires "all one-step reducts in R_{B2}" and SN.
   
   So we need an expansion lemma that says: if all one-step reducts of t are in R_T, then... but that's exactly the "candidate" closure property, which should hold for this R_T if R_T is a reducibility candidate. Indeed, the sets R_T defined this way should be saturated: if t is neutral (not a value) and all its reducts are in R_T, then t ∈ R_T. Hmm, neutral terms like applications: is "all one-step reducts in R_T" enough? For R_{A->B} we need forall u ∈ R_A: t u ∈ R_B. But t's reducts being in R_{A->B} doesn't directly tell us t u ∈ R_B unless... we consider reducts of t u: they're t' u (from t --> t') and possibly beta if t is an abstraction (contradicts neutral). So `t u`'s reducts are `t' u` with `t' ∈ R_{A->B}`, giving `t' u ∈ R_B`. So if t is not a value, LR at arrow can be established from its reducts by the same argument, requiring induction on SN u for ST_App2 cases... but t neutral means ST_App2 requires value t, false unless t is a value. Wait: if t is neutral (app), then t u steps only via ST_App1 (t --> t') — since ST_App2 requires value t and ST_AppAbs requires t an abstraction. So for neutral t, all reducts of t u are t' u. Great, no induction on u needed for neutral t!
   
   So maybe the expansion lemma holds for *neutral* t: if t is not a value (in our language, t is an app or a bvar/fvar... though bvar/fvar aren't really values but also don't reduce; hmm, "neutral" in CBV = not a value). Let's check: if t is not a value, then t is either app, bvar, or fvar (abs/true/false are values). bvar/fvar have no reducts and no typing, but semantically LR is only for lc terms and typed terms; bvar at top-level isn't lc. fvar can be lc but has no step rules; `fvar x u` can step via ST_App1 only if `fvar x --> `, impossible. So app is the only neutral with steps.
   
So define the expansion lemma for terms t where `not_value t := forall t', step t t' -> ...` and where the step relation's applicability for app t u only uses t's steps. Actually the cleanest formulation: 

**Lemma C (candidate closure for non-values):** If `lc t`, `SN t`, `~ value t`, and `forall t', step t t' -> LR T t'`, then `LR T t`.
Proof by induction on T:
- Bool: lc, SN ✓.
- A->B: lc, SN ✓; forall u, LR A u -> LR B (app t u). Apply IH at B? IH at B requires lc, SN, ~value(app t u), and all reducts of app t u in LR B. 
  - ~value (app t u): true since app is not a value (need lemma value_app_inv : value (app t1 t2) -> False).
  - lc ✓.
  - SN (app t u): prove by SN_intro: reducts of app t u:
    * ST_App1: t --> t', reduct app t' u; hypothesis gives LR (A->B) t', arrow clause gives LR B (app t' u) → SN.
    * ST_App2: requires value t, contradicting ~value t.
    * ST_AppAbs: requires t = abs, contradiction ~value... actually abs is a value; ~value t rules it out.
  - reducts of app t u in LR B: as above ST_App1 gives LR B (app t' u) ✓. Others impossible. Then IH at B gives LR B (app t u). But hold on, IH at B is from structural induction on T = A->B. ✓.

So Lemma C works if we have: for `t` non-value, any step of `t u` is either ST_App1 with t --> t', or ST_App2 (impossible since value t false), or ST_AppAbs (impossible since t not abs; but ~value t implies not abs since abs is a value — need value_abs: lc → value). Hmm wait: ST_AppAbs requires t = tm_abs T' t0. If t = abs, then t is a value when lc. But ~value t could hold if abs is not lc. In that case ST_AppAbs could fire if the argument is a value and lc abs — but ST_AppAbs requires `locally_closed (tm_abs T t)`, contradiction with ~value? value (abs T t) is defined as lc (abs T t), so if ST_AppAbs fires, value (abs T t) holds, contradicting ~value t. Good, so ST_AppAbs is ruled out by ~value t via the rule's hypothesis. Similarly ST_App2 requires value t.

Now, for the value/abstraction introduction (Lemma E / the T_Abs case), we have t = abs A t1, and we need `LR (A->B) (abs A t1)`. This needs lc, SN (values are SN — but careful: SN of a value requires no-step lemma), and forall u, LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u). 

For the forall clause with u related: `app (abs A t1) u` is a non-value (app), so Lemma C at B applies if we can show lc, SN, and all reducts in LR B:
- lc ✓
- SN (app abs u): SN_intro; reducts: beta → open t1 u with bc gives LR B → SN; ST_App2 → app abs u' with u --> u', need SN: by SN induction on u (u' smaller), using LR A u' and bc... So we do need SN induction on u here. But now with Lemma C available, we can prove:

**Lemma E:** for all u with LR A u and SN u... Actually let's prove by SN induction on u: for all u, LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u), given lc_at 1 t1 and bc : forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u). Hmm, wait — bc is stated for ALL u related, including u' etc. So in the ST_App2 case, we need LR B (app abs u'). By the IH on SN u' (with LR A u' from LR_step), we get it. And in the beta case, bc gives LR B (open t1 u). But we don't have LR B (app abs u) as an induction hypothesis for the current u; we're proving it via Lemma C at B, which requires all reducts in LR B and SN:
- reducts in LR B: beta case bc ✓; ST_App2 case IH ✓.
- SN (app abs u): SN_intro with the same reduct analysis: beta → LR B → SN; ST_App2 → IH gives LR B (app abs u') → SN. ✓
Therefore Lemma C at B gives LR B (app abs u). But wait, Lemma C requires ~value (app abs u), lc, SN, and all reducts LR B. All available! And Lemma C is proven by induction on B, so calling it at B (a subterm of the current type?) — in Lemma E, B is just some type; we invoke Lemma C at B which is structurally smaller than... nothing. Lemma C is already proven (it's a standalone lemma by induction on T), so no issue. 

But there's a circularity concern: Lemma C's proof by induction on T uses only its own IH at subterms; it doesn't depend on Lemma E. Lemma E uses Lemma C at B. Fine, no circularity.

Wait, in Lemma C at type A->B, the IH at B is used, and the proof needs `LR B (app t u)` derived from the expansion hypothesis at compile time... Let's carefully redo Lemma C:

**Lemma C (LR_expand_neutral):** forall T t, locally_closed t -> SN t -> ~value t -> (forall t', step t t' -> LR T t') -> LR T t.
Proof: induction on T.
- T = Bool: lc, SN.
- T = A -> B: lc, SN, forall u Hu: LR B (app t u).
  Apply IH_B with term (app t u): need lc (app t u) ✓; SN (app t u); ~value (app t u) ✓; forall x, step (app t u) x -> LR B x.
  - SN (app t u): SN_intro; steps:
    * ST_AppAbs: requires t = abs and lc abs, hence value t — contradiction with ~value t. Wait: ST_AppAbs's hypotheses are lc (abs T' t0) and value v, giving app (abs T' t0) v --> open. For inversion to match `app t u`, need t = abs T' t0, and then lc (abs T' t0), so value t. Contradiction ✓.
    * ST_App1: t --> t' and lc u; result app t' u. We have LR (A->B) t' from the hypothesis, so LR B (app t' u) ✓, so SN.
    * ST_App2: requires value t, contradiction ✓.
  - forall x, step (app t u) x -> LR B x: same cases; ST_App1 gives LR B (app t' u) ✓; others contradiction.
  - Then IH_B gives LR B (app t u). ✓
  Done.

Great, this works and doesn't need SN induction on u because app t u only steps through t (neutral). 

**Lemma E (application of an abstraction):** Given lc_at 1 t1, bc : forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u):
  forall u, LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u).
Proof by induction on SN u (i.e., well-founded induction on the SN derivation). Hmm, careful: The needed induction is on u with the statement P u := LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u). We need to also handle the beta case which uses bc directly. Let me define the lemma via `strongly_normalizing_ind` or Acc:

```
Lemma E : forall A B t1, lc_at 1 t1 -> (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) ->
  forall u, LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u).
Proof.
  intros A B t1 Hlc Hbc u Hu.
  (* strong induction on SN u *)
  remember (LR_sn A u Hu) as Hsn eqn:E.
  revert u Hu Hsn... 
```
Standard: `induction Hsn` using the SN induction principle, generalizing the statement. Let me define P u := forall A B t1, lc_at 1 t1 -> (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) -> LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u). Hmm, but in the step case, we need the IH for reducts u' with LR A u' and the same A B t1 — fine since P is generalized over A B t1.

Wait, but there's a subtlety: in the ST_App2 case, the reduct is app (abs A t1) u' and we need LR B of it. IH at u' requires SN u' (available from SN_step) and LR A u' (from LR_step). ✓. In the beta case, we need LR B (open t1 u) which is Hbc u Hu ✓.

Then to conclude LR B (app abs u), use Lemma C at B with:
- lc (app abs u): lc abs from lc_at 1 t1, lc u from LR_lc ✓.
- SN (app abs u): SN_intro with reduct analysis: beta → LR B (open t1 u) → SN; ST_App2 → IH at u' gives LR B (app abs u') → SN; ST_App1 impossible; ST_AppAbs same as beta. ✓
- ~value (app abs u) ✓.
- all reducts in LR B: beta → Hbc ✓; ST_App2 → IH ✓. Others impossible.
✓.

Great. Note Lemma E's induction hypothesis usage requires that in the ST_App2 case, the step is `u --> u'`; then IH (from SN induction) applies to u' because `step u u'` and SN u. In Coq, the SN induction principle: 
`SN_ind : forall P, (forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) -> forall t, SN t -> P t` (if generated). Then `induction Hsn` with Hsn : SN u gives the IH `forall u', step u u' -> P u'`. Good.

Now, what about the `LR A u -> LR A u'` (LR_step at A) and `lc u'`? LR_step needs `step u u'`, which we have.

**Back to the fundamental theorem.**

`sem_typing Gamma t T := forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst_fvar sigma t)`.

Wait, do we need `Gamma` in LR? LR is unary closed; env_ok maps Gamma-assigned atoms to LR terms. Let's define:

```
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x).
```

Then:
**Fundamental theorem:** `forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t)`.

Proof by induction on the typing derivation.
- T_Var: Gamma x = Some T. env_ok gives LR T (sigma x) = LR T (subst sigma (fvar x)). ✓
- T_True/T_False: need LR Bool true = lc true ∧ SN true. lc ✓ (lc_true). SN true: values are SN. ✓. But wait, subst sigma true = true. ✓.
- T_App: IH1: forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR (T1->T2) (subst sigma t1). IH2: ... -> LR T1 (subst sigma t2). Goal: LR T2 (subst sigma (app t1 t2)) = LR T2 (app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)). From IH1 applied to sigma: LR (T1->T2) (subst t1); its arrow clause applied to (subst t2) with IH2: LR T2 (app ...). ✓
- T_Abs: Goal: LR (T1->T2) (subst sigma (abs T1 t1)) = LR (T1->T2) (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)).
  We have Hbody : forall x, ~In x L -> has_type (x|->T1; Gamma) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2.
  IHbody : forall x, ~In x L -> forall sigma', env_ok (x|->T1; Gamma) sigma' -> LR T2 (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))).
  Need: lc (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)), SN (abs ...), and forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) u).
  
  - lc (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) = lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1). We know from typing? Need a lemma: typing implies lc, and substitution preserves lc. Hmm. We need `lc_at 1 (subst_fvar sigma t1)` where sigma maps Gamma atoms to LR terms (all lc). But Gamma under which t1 is typed? t1 is the body of the lambda typed under `x|->T1;Gamma` (after opening with fvar x). Hmm. We need a general lemma: if `has_type Delta t T` and sigma is lc on Delta's atoms (all sigma x for x in Delta are lc) then lc (subst sigma t). Or simpler: first prove `typing_lc : has_type Gamma t T -> lc t` and then use `lc_subst`: lc t and (forall x in fvars t, lc (sigma x)) -> lc (subst sigma t). But we don't know that sigma is lc on all fvars of t1 from Gamma alone... env_ok gives LR (hence lc) for atoms in Gamma; t1's free fvars — under `x|->T1;Gamma`, t1 may have free fvars only among those (if typing_lc holds, fvars are "typed"). Hmm, need more.
  
    Actually, here's a cleaner way: the T_Abs case can get `lc (abs T1 (subst sigma t1))` from the IH at some fresh x with sigma' x = some lc term? No — the IH gives LR T2 of `subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))`, whose lc implies... hmm, we'd need to invert substitution. Ugh.
    
    Alternative: prove a general lemma `sem_typing_lc : has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> locally_closed (subst sigma t)`. This can be proven by induction on the typing derivation *simultaneously* with the fundamental theorem (or as a corollary if we define env_ok to require lc, and prove lc preservation). Let's see: T_Abs case: need lc (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) = lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1). Hmm, but t1 is not typed directly; only `open t1 (fvar x)` is typed. So we'd need the same opening-inversion issue.
    
    Better: define the semantic judgment to include lc: `sem Gamma t T sigma := locally_closed (subst sigma t) /\ LR T (subst sigma t)`? Then induction would give lc for the body's substitution, but the T_Abs case still needs lc of subst sigma t1 from lc of subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)). Hmm.

  Let's think about how to get `lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1)`. We can prove a separate lemma by induction on typing derivations that gives lc of open terms, then relate. Actually, maybe simplest: prove **`typing_lc_open`**: `has_type Gamma t T -> lc t` by induction on typing (as discussed, T_Abs uses the opening inversion). Also prove **`lc_subst`**: if `lc t` and for all x in fvars t, lc (sigma x), then lc (subst sigma t). But we need to know which fvars t1 has and that sigma is lc there. Since `has_type (x|->T1; Gamma) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2` and x fresh from t1, the fvars of open t1 (fvar x) = fvars t1 ∪ {x} (since t1 has no bvar 0 free at top? hmm, opening replaces bvar 0 with fvar x; the free fvars of open t1 (fvar x) = fvars t1 ∪ {x} if bvar 0 occurs, else fvars t1). And typing_lc gives lc of it, but we need lc of sigma applied to those fvars.

Hmm, this is getting complicated. Let's step back and think about whether we can avoid the environment/substitution approach for lc, by getting lc from the *typing judgment* directly in a way compatible with the T_Abs case.

Observation: In the T_Abs case, the goal's lc requirement is `lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1)`. We can obtain this if we can prove a lemma: for any term t1 and any sigma, if for all x, `(forall T', ...)`... Let's try to prove `lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1)` from the hypothesis that for a fresh x (x ∉ fvars t1 ∪ L), `locally_closed (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)))` where sigma' x = some lc term and sigma' agrees with sigma elsewhere. Then `subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) (sigma' x)` (using freshness of x in t1). Hmm, we know lc of `open (subst sigma t1) u` for a particular lc u, and want lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1). This is the opening inversion lemma applied to subst sigma t1 with u = sigma' x. So we need:
`lc_open_inv : forall t u, locally_closed u -> locally_closed (open t u) -> lc_at 1 t`.
That's the lemma we discussed: `lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 u t) -> lc_at 0 u -> lc_at 1 t`. ✓ provable.

So we need:

`step_lc` proof details:
```
Lemma step_lc : forall t t', step t t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' H. induction H; intros Hlc; [ | | ].
  - (* ST_AppAbs *) 
    (* t = app (abs T t0) v, t' = open t0 v *)
    inversion Hlc as ... (* lc_app with lc (abs T t0) and lc v *)
    apply lc_open_rec with (k:=0); [ lc_at 1 t0 from lc_abs | lc v ].
  - (* ST_App1 *)
    inversion Hlc; subst. apply lc_app; [ apply IHstep; assumption | assumption using H2? ].
    Wait, the rule's side condition gives locally_closed t2. Good.
  - (* ST_App2 *)
    inversion Hlc; subst; apply lc_app; [ assumption? | apply IHstep; assumption ].
Qed.
```
Careful with variable naming and the induction: `induction H` where H : step t t', with a goal containing `locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'`. Better to do `intros t t' H; induction H; intros Hlc`. The IH for ST_App1 is `locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1'`. Fine.

But wait: there's a subtlety in the induction: the fundamental theorem statement is
`forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t)`.
Induction on the typing derivation `H : has_type Gamma t T`. The IH for T_Abs's premise: since the premise is `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`, the induction hypothesis must be for that premise instance — Coq's induction on the inductive derivation: T_Abs's argument is a function `forall x, ~In x L -> has_type ...`. The generated IH will be `forall x Hfresh, forall sigma, env_ok (x|->T1;Gamma) sigma -> LR T2 (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x)))` — hopefully. Since the recursive occurrence is under a forall and an implication, Coq should generate IH as a function. Let's test: the induction principle for has_type. T_Abs constructor: `forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2) -> has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2)`. The induction principle generated by Coq for this: P Gamma t T, with the T_Abs case requiring `forall L Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x H, P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) -> P Gamma (abs T1 t1) (T1->T2)`. Coq does generate such IH (the recursive occurrence under a function type is allowed; the predicate P is applied to the recursive argument). Yes, Coq handles this since the inductive occurrence is strictly positive: `(forall x, ~In x L -> has_type ...)` — the recursive occurrence appears as the conclusion of a function type argument... Hmm, strictly positive? `has_type` occurs in the type of the argument to the constructor. The occurrence `has_type (update ...) ... T2` is in the codomain of a forall, which is a positive position? For strict positivity, occurrences in argument types of constructors must be positive; here the argument has type `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...`; the occurrence of has_type is in the codomain of an arrow within the constructor argument type. Coq's positivity checker: an inductive occurrence in the type of a constructor argument is allowed if it's strictly positive; `(forall x, A -> I)` as an argument type — the occurrence of I is in a positive position (double negation-like: argument of the constructor is a function returning I, which is a positive occurrence? The rule: I may occur strictly positively; in `forall x, P -> I`, I occurs as the conclusion, which is a strictly positive position). Yes, this pattern is standard in SF, and Coq generates the induction principle with the IH being `forall x H, P ...`.

```coq
(* after the supplied definitions *)

(** Substitution for free variables (locally nameless: no capture issues). *)
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition id_subst : atom -> tm := fun x => tm_fvar x.

Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst id_subst t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; auto.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2; reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt; reflexivity.
Qed.
```
Careful: for tm_app, `simpl; auto` might not use IH if the statement is about subst id t = t; after simpl, goal is `tm_app (subst id t1) (subst id t2) = tm_app t1 t2`; `rewrite IHt1, IHt2; reflexivity`. For bvar/fvar: fvar case: subst id (fvar x) = id x = fvar x ✓ (simpl reduces). bvar ✓.

```coq
(** Free variables *)
Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true | tm_false => nil
  end.
```
Hmm, the `| tm_true | tm_false => nil` pattern syntax is allowed in Coq for Fixpoint? Yes with multiple patterns.

Lemma subst_open_rec : forall sigma, (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) ->
  forall k u t, subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t).

  - (* fvar *)

```coq
Lemma SN_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.

Actually, let me think about the guard condition for `SN_Acc`:
```
Fixpoint SN_Acc (t : tm) (h : strongly_normalizing t) {struct h} : Acc step t :=
  match h with
  | SN_intro t' f => Acc_intro t' (fun y hy => SN_Acc y (f y hy))
  end.
```
The recursive call `SN_Acc y (f y hy)`: the structural argument is `f y hy`, which is not a subterm of h in the syntactic sense. Coq's guard checker will reject this. So use the generated induction principle instead:
```
Lemma SN_Acc : forall t, strongly_normalizing t -> Acc step t.
Proof.
  intros t H. induction H as [t Hred IH].
  apply Acc_intro. intros t' Hstep. apply IH. exact Hstep.
Qed.
```
Here `induction H` uses the generated induction principle `strongly_normalizing_ind` with IH : forall t', step t t' -> Acc step t'. This should work if Coq generates the principle with the function premise handled. Coq's scheme generation: For `SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> SN t') -> SN t`, the induction principle is
`strongly_normalizing_ind : forall P : tm -> Prop, (forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) -> forall t, SN t -> P t`? 
I believe Coq generates this because the recursive occurrence is in a strictly positive position under the arrow. The generated scheme for such "nested" occurrences inside products functions: Coq's `Scheme` can handle `forall` and `->` in constructor arguments. Yes, e.g., `Inductive le : nat -> nat -> Prop := le_n : forall n, le n n | le_S : forall n m, le n m -> le (S n) m` no; better example: `Inductive clos_refl_trans : ...` has a constructor `forall x y z, R x y -> clos_refl_trans y z -> ...`. Or `Inductive Acc` itself: `Acc_intro : forall A R x, (forall y, R y x -> Acc R y) -> Acc R x` and its induction principle is well-formed (Acc_ind). So yes, Coq handles it: `Acc_ind : forall A R P, (forall x, (forall y, R y x -> P y) -> P x) -> forall x, Acc R x -> P x`. Similarly strongly_normalizing_ind should be generated.

Let me now write Lemma C and E carefully.

```coq
Definition LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```
Note: since LR is defined by recursion on T, Coq needs it as a Fixpoint or Definition with a match. Using `Definition LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop := match T with ... end` is fine but Coq may complain about the recursive call? There's no recursion on t; the recursive calls are on T1 and T2 which are subterms of T. As a plain Definition with match, Coq requires structural recursion for `Fixpoint`, but here LR is used inside its own body — so it must be a Fixpoint? Actually `Definition LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop := match T with ... LR T1 u ... end` — this is a recursive definition but written as a Definition; Coq will reject (LR not defined yet). We need `Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop := match T with ... end`. Yes, since T is an inductive, this works — the recursive calls are `LR T1 u` and `LR T2 (tm_app t u)` with T1, T2 subterms of T. ✓ But note the recursive call `LR T1 u` has a different term argument u, which is fine (structural recursion on T only).

Then LR unfolding: `simpl` or `unfold LR` should reduce when T is a constructor. With `Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm)`, `simpl` on `LR (Ty_Arrow A B) x` reduces. Good.

Now Lemma E:

```coq
Lemma LR_abs_app : forall A B (t1 : tm),
  lc_at 1 t1 ->
  (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) ->
  forall u, LR A u -> LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A t1) u).

Hmm, using the generated induction principle with a general motive: We want P u := forall A B t1, lc_at 1 t1 -> (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) -> LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u). ```

pose proof (LR_sn A u Hu) as Hsn.

Since we reverted A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta Hu before induction, the motive is `fun u => forall A B t1, lc_at 1 t1 -> ... -> LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u)`? Hmm, careful: Hu : LR A u appears after u; when we revert, the order matters. Let's revert all: `revert A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta Hu; induction Hsn`. Then the goal is `forall A B t1, lc_at 1 t1 -> (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) -> LR A u0 -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u0)` and the IH should be `forall u', step u0 u' -> (forall A B t1, ... -> LR A u' -> ...)`. Coq generates the IH by applying the motive to each reduct. I think this works: strongly_normalizing_ind with P u := forall A B t1, ... LR A u -> ...; the induction step gives `(forall u', step u0 u' -> P u')`. ✓

Then in the proof:
- Goal: LR B (app (abs A t1) u0), given Hu : LR A u0.
- Apply LR_neutral_expand at B with t := app (abs A t1) u0:
  * lc: `apply lc_app; [apply lc_abs; exact Hlc1 | apply LR_lc with (T:=A); exact Hu]`.
    locally_closed (abs A t1) = lc_at 0 (abs A t1), and lc_abs : forall k T t, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k (abs T t); with k=0 gives lc_at 1 t1 ✓.
  * SN: `apply SN_intro; intros t' Hst`. Analyze Hst.

Prove by `inversion 1`? value has constructors v_abs, v_true, v_false; none matches app → inversion should close. Let's prove `Lemma value_app_inv : forall t1 t2, value (tm_app t1 t2) -> False. Proof. intros t1 t2 H; inversion H. Qed.`
  * forall t', step (app abs u0) t' -> LR B t'. Same analysis.
  
  The reduct analysis: `inversion Hst as [T0 t0 v Hlc0 Hv0 | t1' t1'' t2 Hstep1 Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv1 Hstep2]; subst`.
  - ST_AppAbs: t' = open t0 v0 with abs A t1 = abs T0 t0 → A = T0, t1 = t0, and v0 = u0. Then t' = open t1 u0 and Hbeta u0 Hu gives LR B ✓.
  - ST_App1: step (abs A t1) t1' — impossible: need `abs_no_step : forall A t t', step (tm_abs A t) t' -> False`. Prove by inversion.
  - ST_App2: value (abs A t1) (given by the rule), u0 --> t2', t' = app (abs A t1) t2'. Apply IH: IH t2' Hstep2 A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta (LR_step A u0 t2' Hu Hstep2) ✓.
  
  Wait, for ST_App2, after inversion the argument step is `u0 --> t2'`; IH is `forall u', step u0 u' -> P u'`; so `apply (IH t2' Hstep2 A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta)` then need LR A t2' from LR_step ✓.

Note: in the SN subgoal, the ST_App2 case uses IH to get LR B (app abs t2') and then LR_sn. In the "all reducts" subgoal, same. Good.

But there's a subtlety: `LR_neutral_expand` at B requires `strongly_normalizing (app (abs A t1) u0)`, which we prove by SN_intro using the reduct analysis; the reduct analysis in the ST_App2 case uses IH, which is fine (SN induction). ✓ No circularity: IH is for reducts u' with step u0 u'.

Hmm, but note: `LR_neutral_expand` requires `~ value t` and `lc t`, `SN t`, and all reducts LR. All proven.

Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write the definition of env_ok:

```coq
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x)).
```

Fundamental theorem:
```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H; intros sigma Henv.
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. destruct Henv as [Hlc Hmap]. apply Hmap. assumption.
  - (* T_Abs *) simpl. 
    (* goal: LR (T1 -> T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) *)
    split; [| split].
    + (* lc *) apply lc_abs. 
      (* need lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1) *)
      ...
    + (* SN *) apply value_SN. apply v_abs. apply lc_abs. ...
    + (* forall u *) intros u Hu. apply LR_abs_app with (t1 := subst sigma t1).
      * lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1)
      * intros u' Hu'. (* beta-reduct *) ...
      * exact Hu.
  - (* T_App *) simpl. destruct (IHhas_type1 sigma Henv) as ...? 
```
Careful: the IH names. `induction H` where H : has_type Gamma t T. The constructors: T_Var, T_Abs, T_App, T_True, T_False. The IH for T_App's two premises: IHhas_type1, IHhas_type2. But the premises have different Gamma, t, T — the induction principle is generated for the specific instance? Since the motive is `fun Gamma t T => forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t)`, the IHs would be:
- IHhas_type1 : forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T1 (subst sigma t1)
- IHhas_type2 : forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T1'? ... 
Actually with the motive applied to the premises: for premise `<{ Gamma |-- t1 \in T1 -> T2 }>`, IH = `forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR (T1 -> T2) (subst sigma t1)`. ✓
- T_Abs: IH = forall x, ~ In x L -> forall sigma, env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma -> LR T2 (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))). ✓ (assuming Coq generates this as a function).
- T_App: IH1 : forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR (T1->T2) (subst sigma t1); IH2 : forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T1 (subst sigma t2). Goal: LR T2 (subst sigma (app t1 t2)) = LR T2 (app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)) (after simpl). Apply IH1 sigma Henv → LR (T1->T2) (subst sigma t1); then its arrow clause with (IH2 sigma Henv).
```
    + (* T_App *)
      simpl. pose proof (IHhas_type1 sigma Henv) as H1. pose proof (IHhas_type2 sigma Henv) as H2.
      simpl in H1. destruct H1 as [Hlc1 [Hsn1 Happ1]]. apply Happ1. exact H2.
```
Hmm, `simpl in H1`: if T1 is an arrow type then LR (T1->T2) unfolds; but T1 is an arbitrary type variable here. H1 : LR (T1 -> T2) (subst sigma t1) where T1, T2 are variables (the constructor's parameters) — `LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) x` should reduce by simpl since Ty_Arrow T1 T2 is a constructor application. ✓
- T_True/T_False: simpl; split; [apply lc_true | apply value_SN; constructor]. Note locally_closed tm_true = lc_at 0 tm_true ✓ lc_true 0.

Hmm wait: for T_True, the goal after simpl is `LR Ty_Bool tm_true` (since subst sigma tm_true = tm_true) → unfold LR → `locally_closed tm_true /\ SN tm_true` ✓.

Now the T_Abs case in detail. Context:
- Gamma : context, L : list atom, T1 T2 : ty, t1 : tm.
- Hbody : forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2.
- IHbody : forall x, ~ In x L -> forall sigma, env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma -> LR T2 (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))).
- sigma : atom -> tm, Henv : env_ok Gamma sigma.
Goal: LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)).

Let's write:
```
  - (* T_Abs *)
    simpl.
    (* choose fresh x *)
    set (l := L ++ fvars t1).
    set (x := fresh_atom l).  (* fresh_atom l = S (max_list l) *)
    assert (HfreshL : ~ In x L) by (apply not_in_app_... ).
    assert (Hfresht1 : ~ occurs_free x t1).
    (* lc of body *)
    assert (Hlcbody : locally_closed (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x)))).
    { apply LR_lc with (T := T2). apply IHbody; [exact HfreshL | apply env_ok_update_fvar ...]. }
```
Wait: IHbody x HfreshL sigma' Henv' where sigma' = update_env x (fvar x) sigma. We get LR T2 (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))). We need locally_closed (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))) — hmm, we need to show subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) = subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))? Since x is not free in t1, and open t1 (fvar x) has fvar x occurrences where bvar 0 occurred. sigma' x = fvar x, while sigma x might be something else. But wait: `subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))` — the fvar x in this term (introduced by opening) would be replaced by sigma x under sigma! Hmm, but we want `subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))` to equal `open (subst sigma t1) (fvar x)`? Let's compute: subst sigma (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) = open_rec 0 (subst sigma (fvar x)) (subst sigma t1) = open_rec 0 (sigma x) (subst sigma t1). That's NOT open_rec 0 (fvar x) (subst sigma t1) unless sigma x = fvar x.

I need to be careful here. The lc goal is `lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1)` (for lc of abs). We want to derive it from LR T2 (open (subst sigma t1) (fvar x)). To get that from the IH, we need sigma' with sigma' x = fvar x AND sigma' = sigma elsewhere, and then:
subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open_rec 0 (subst sigma' (fvar x)) (subst sigma' t1) = open_rec 0 (fvar x) (subst sigma t1) = open (subst sigma t1) (fvar x). ✓
So Hlcbody should be about `subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))` with sigma' x = fvar x, giving lc (open (subst sigma t1) (fvar x)) → lc_open_inv → lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1). ✓

So define sigma_x := fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then tm_fvar x else sigma y.
Then:
- env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma_x:
  * lc: for y = x: lc (fvar x) ✓; else sigma y lc ✓.
  * map: for y T, update Gamma x T1 y = Some T -> LR T (sigma_x y).
    - y = x: update = Some T1 so T = T1 (need to show), sigma_x x = fvar x, LR T1 (fvar x) by LR_fvar ✓.
    - y ≠ x: update y = Gamma y, sigma_x y = sigma y, use Henv ✓.
- IHbody x HfreshL sigma_x (env_ok ...) : LR T2 (subst sigma_x (open t1 (fvar x))).
- Show subst sigma_x (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) (fvar x):
  rewrite subst_open_rec (needs lc of all sigma_x images ✓).
  subst sigma_x (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) = open_rec 0 (subst sigma_x (fvar x)) (subst sigma_x t1) = open_rec 0 (fvar x) (subst sigma_x t1).
  And subst sigma_x t1 = subst sigma t1 by subst_ext (x not free in t1).
  So = open_rec 0 (fvar x) (subst sigma t1) = open (subst sigma t1) (fvar x) ✓.
- Then lc (open (subst sigma t1) (fvar x)) from LR_lc T2, and lc_open_inv with u = fvar x: lc (fvar x) ✓ gives lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1) ✓.

Good. Also the SN part: value (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) requires lc (abs ...) = lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1) ✓, then value_SN.

And the beta property for LR_abs_app: for each u with Hu : LR T1 u, define sigma_u := fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y. Then:
- env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma_u: lc: u lc from LR_lc ✓; map: y=x → LR T1 u ✓; else ✓.
- IHbody x HfreshL sigma_u → LR T2 (subst sigma_u (open t1 (fvar x))).
- subst sigma_u (open t1 (fvar x)) = open_rec 0 (subst sigma_u (fvar x)) (subst sigma_u t1) = open_rec 0 u (subst sigma t1) = open (subst sigma t1) u ✓ (using subst_ext: x not free in t1).
- So LR T2 (open (subst sigma t1) u) ✓.

Then LR_abs_app T1 T2 (subst sigma t1) Hlc1 Hbeta u Hu gives LR T2 (app (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) u) — and the goal in the forall clause is LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) u) ✓.

Great. Note: LR_abs_app requires `lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1)` (Hlc1).

Now, the fresh atom lemma:
```coq
Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof. induction l as [|a l IH]; intros x H; simpl in H. contradiction. 
  destruct H as [<- | H]. apply Nat.le_max_l. apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list l); [apply IH; assumption | apply Nat.le_max_r]. Qed.

Definition fresh_atom (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_list l).

Lemma fresh_atom_spec : forall l, ~ In (fresh_atom l) l.
Proof. intros l H. unfold fresh_atom in H. pose proof (max_list_spec l (S (max_list l)) H). lia. Qed.
```
max_list lives in a namespace? `max_list` might clash with List's if imported. Let's call it `max_atoms` to be safe. Also `Nat.max` lemmas: `Nat.le_max_l : forall n m, n <= max n m`, `Nat.le_max_r`. ✓

`In` and `app`: L ++ fvars t1. `~ In x (L ++ fvars t1)` gives both by `In_app_iff` or `app_iff`. Use:
```
Lemma not_in_app : forall (A : Type) (x : A) l1 l2, ~ In x (l1 ++ l2) -> ~ In x l1 /\ ~ In x l2.
Proof. intros; split; intro H; apply H; apply in_or_app; auto. Qed.
```
Or use `List.in_app_iff`. Let's just prove our own simple lemma or use `apply in_app_iff` in the forward direction: `H : ~ In x (l1 ++ l2)`; `intro H1; apply H; apply in_app_iff; left; assumption`. Fine.

Lemma update_eq : forall Gamma x T, update Gamma x T x = Some T.

Lemma update_neq : forall Gamma x y T, x <> y -> update Gamma x T y = Gamma y.
Proof. intros. unfold update. apply Nat.eqb_neq in H. rewrite H. reflexivity. Qed.
```
Nat.eqb_neq : forall n m, n <> m -> (n =? m) = false. ✓

Now the final theorem:
```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  apply LR_sn with (T := T).
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty id_subst) as H.
  assert (Henv : env_ok empty id_subst).
  { split; [intro x; apply lc_fvar | intros x T0 Hx; unfold empty in Hx; discriminate]. }
  specialize (H Henv).
  rewrite subst_id in H. exact H.
Qed.
```
Note `empty` is a Definition taking an atom and returning None: `empty x = None` by definition; `unfold empty in Hx; discriminate` ✓.

Wait, `fundamental` is stated with `has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t)`. To apply, we need `has_type empty t T`, which is the hypothesis `<{ empty |-- t \in T }>` — the notation is `has_type Gamma t T`. Since it's parsed via custom entry, should be definitionally equal after `simpl` maybe. The hypothesis Hty : <{ empty |-- t \in T }> which unfolds to has_type empty t T? The notation is just a reserved notation for has_type, so Hty : has_type empty t T syntactically. ✓

Let me also consider: do we need `SN` of `tm_true` and `tm_false`? value_SN covers.

We need a lemma for `app (app (fvar x) u) w` — a doubly-stuck term. Generalize: for any list of arguments? Hmm, alternatively prove by induction on B:
`LR_stuck : forall B (t : tm), locally_closed t -> (forall t', step t t' -> False) -> LR B t`.
That is: any stuck (no-step), locally closed term is in LR at every type! Is that true? For B = Bool: lc ∧ SN ✓. For B = B1->B2: lc ∧ SN ∧ forall u, LR B1 u -> LR B2 (app t u). A value has no steps, but if t is a value, app t u could beta-reduce (if t = abs and u value) — but that requires t = abs which is a value, and our hypothesis "no steps" doesn't rule out t being an abstraction. Hmm, e.g., t = abs T (bvar 0) has no steps (as a standalone term), but app t u steps. So the statement is false for arbitrary no-step t. However, for t = fvar x, `value (fvar x)` is false, so app (fvar x) u has no steps. Let's prove the stronger specific lemma:

`LR_stuck_app : forall B x (args : list tm)?` Or simply prove by induction on B with a generalized statement: for all terms t that are "headed by a free variable with arguments",... 

Cleaner: prove a lemma that any term of the form `app (... (app (fvar x) u1) ... un)` is LR at any type, by induction on the type B, where the induction step needs the term `app (that term) w` — which is of the same shape with one more argument! So state it over a *list of arguments*:

`Lemma LR_fvar_spine : forall B x args, Forall locally_closed args -> LR B (apply_spine (fvar x) args)`.

where apply_spine builds nested apps. Then for the arrow case, the forall-w clause needs `app (apply_spine (fvar x) args) w = apply_spine (fvar x) (args ++ [w])`, and lc of args ++ [w] from lc w. Induction on B. For the SN part, need "no steps" of a fvar-headed spine: prove `step_spine_fvar : forall x args t', step (apply_spine (fvar x) args) t' -> False` by induction on args (with the app_fvar... hmm, the head changes as we nest: after one app, the head is `app (fvar x) u1`, and we need that it doesn't step: ST_App1 requires fvar x --> (no), ST_App2 requires value (fvar x) (no), ST_AppAbs requires fvar x = abs (no). So indeed the whole spine has no steps. Prove by induction on args:
- []: fvar x no step ✓.
- a :: args: the term is apply_spine (app (fvar x) a) args... hmm, the orientation: apply_spine t [a1; a2] = app (app t a1) a2. Induction: for apply_spine t (a :: args) = apply_spine (app t a) args, need no-step of (app t a) where t is the head spine... We need: if t = apply_spine (fvar x) prefix has no steps, then app t a has no steps? No — that's false in general (if t is an abs). But here t is fvar-headed; we need that it's not a value and its no-step property. Let's define a predicate "stuck":

`Definition stuck (t : tm) : Prop := locally_closed t /\ (forall t', step t t' -> False) /\ ~ value t.`

Hmm, but ~ value is needed to rule out ST_App2 (which requires value of the head). Then if stuck t, app t u has no steps and is not a value. But for LR at arrow, we'd need the forall clause recursively. Let's define:

`LR_stuck : forall B t, locally_closed t -> ~ value t -> (forall t', step t t' -> False) -> LR B t`? 

Check arrow case: given lc t, ~value t, no-step t. Want forall u, LR B1 u -> LR B2 (app t u). Apply IH at B2 with term app t u: lc ✓; ~value (app t u) ✓; no-step (app t u): ST_App1 needs t --> (none ✓); ST_App2 needs value t (contradiction ✓); ST_AppAbs needs t = abs T t0 with lc — then value t by v_abs, contradiction ✓. So no-step ✓. Then IH at B2 gives LR B2 (app t u) ✓. 

And for the fvar case: fvar x is lc ✓, ~value (fvar x) ✓ (value constructors are abs/true/false), no-step ✓. So `LR_fvar` follows from `LR_stuck` with t = fvar x. And `LR_stuck_app` for app (fvar x) u: app (fvar x) u is lc ✓, ~value ✓, no-step ✓ — so LR at any type ✓. 

So prove `LR_stuck : forall B t, locally_closed t -> ~ value t -> (forall t', step t t' -> False) -> LR B t` by induction on B. Then LR_fvar is a corollary. This is clean.

Proof of LR_stuck:
```coq
Lemma LR_stuck : forall B t,
  locally_closed t -> ~ value t -> (forall t', step t t' -> False) -> LR B t.
Proof.
  induction B as [| B1 IH1 B2 IH2]; intros t Hlc Hnv Hns; simpl.
  - split; [exact Hlc | apply SN_intro; intros t' Hst; exfalso; apply Hns; exact Hst].
  - split; [exact Hlc | split].
    + apply SN_intro; intros t' Hst; exfalso; apply Hns; exact Hst.
    + intros u Hu. apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).
      * apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply LR_lc with (T := B1); exact Hu].
      * intro Hv; apply value_app_inv in Hv; exact Hv.
      * intros t' Hst. inversion Hst as [T0 t0 v Hlc0 Hv0 | t1 t1' t2 Hstep1 Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv1 Hstep2]; subst.
        -- (* ST_AppAbs *) exfalso; apply Hnv. apply v_abs. exact Hlc0.
        -- (* ST_App1 *) exfalso; apply Hns with (t' := t1'). exact Hstep1.
        -- (* ST_App2 *) exfalso; apply Hnv. exact Hv1.
Qed.
```
Hmm, in the ST_AppAbs case, after subst, the head t was unified with abs T0 t0; the hypothesis Hlc0 : locally_closed (abs T0 t0) and Hnv : ~ value t. But after subst t := abs T0 t0? Let me think: inversion Hst where Hst : step (app t u) t'. The constructor ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, lc (abs T t) -> value v -> app (abs T t) v --> open t v. Unifying app t u with app (abs T0 t0) v gives t = abs T0 t0 and u = v, and t' = open t0 v. So after subst, Hnv : ~ value (abs T0 t0), Hlc0 : lc (abs T0 t0); apply v_abs Hlc0 gives value (abs T0 t0), contradiction ✓.

Good. Note this proof's `inversion Hst as [...]` with constructor argument naming. Might need tuning.

Lemma LR_fvar : forall T x, LR T (tm_fvar x).

```

Now `LR_abs_app` in detail:

```coq
Lemma LR_abs_app : forall A B (t1 : tm),
  lc_at 1 t1 ->
  (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) ->
  forall u, LR A u -> LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A t1) u).

  intros A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta u Hu.
  remember (LR_sn A u Hu) as Hsn eqn:Eqsn.
  revert A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta Hu Eqsn.   (* hmm, Eqsn depends on Hu... *)

Hmm, `remember (LR_sn A u Hu) as Hsn` creates a dependency. Simpler: we don't need to remember; we can do `generalize dependent u`? We need to induct on the SN proof of u while keeping u fixed. The standard pattern: 
```
  intros A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta u Hu.
  pose proof (LR_sn A u Hu) as Hsn.
  revert A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta Hu.
  induction Hsn as [u0 Hred IH].
  intros A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta Hu.
```
Wait, after `revert A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta Hu`, the goal is `forall A B t1, lc_at 1 t1 -> (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) -> LR A u0 -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u0)` — with `u0` the term from Hsn. But `Hsn : strongly_normalizing u`, and after revert, u is still in the context (u is not reverted!). Hmm: `pose proof (LR_sn A u Hu) as Hsn` where Hsn : SN u. Then `revert A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta Hu` leaves context with u : tm and Hsn : SN u, goal `forall A B t1, ... -> LR A u -> ...`. Then `induction Hsn` — but the goal mentions u, which is a variable in context, and Hsn : SN u. Induction on Hsn with u fixed: `induction Hsn as [u0 Hred IH]` will generalize the goal over u (since the inductive is indexed by u) — Coq's induction will abstract u in the goal, giving `forall A B t1, ... -> LR A u0 -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u0)`, with IH : `forall u', step u0 u' -> (forall A B t1, ... -> LR A u' -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u'))`. I believe Coq handles this (`induction Hsn` generalizes the index automatically if the proof is in the context and the goal depends on it). Yes, when you `induction H` where H : P u and the goal mentions u, Coq will revert u automatically or produce the generalized induction. It should work.

But actually, there might be a subtlety: after `pose proof ... as Hsn`, the goal still contains `u`; `induction Hsn` will try to abstract occurrences of u in the goal. Since u appears in the goal (`LR A u -> LR B (app (abs A t1) u)`) and Hsn's type `SN u` determines the index, Coq's `induction` will generalize. I think it works. If not, we can use `remember u as u0` or `generalize dependent u` before pose. Let's write it carefully in the file and let the compiler guide.

```coq
Lemma env_ok_update : forall Gamma x T1 (sigma sigma' : atom -> tm),
  env_ok Gamma sigma ->
  locally_closed (sigma' x) ->
  LR T1 (sigma' x) ->
  (forall y, y <> x -> sigma' y = sigma y) ->
  env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma'.
Proof.
  intros Gamma x T1 sigma sigma' [Hlc Hmap] Hlcx HLR Hagree. split.
  - intros y. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + (* not needed *) apply Hlcx? no, sigma' y = sigma' x when y = x *)
```
Hmm, the lc part: for y = x, sigma' y = sigma' x, lc via Hlcx; for y ≠ x, sigma' y = sigma y via Hagree, lc via Hlc. Need Nat.eqb_eq/neq to convert E into y = x or y ≠ x. Let's write:
```
  - intros y. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst y; exact Hlcx.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. rewrite Hagree by exact E. apply Hlc. 
```

```
  - intros y T Hy. unfold update in Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst y. simpl in Hy. (* Hy : Some T1 = Some T *) inversion Hy; subst. exact HLR.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. rewrite E in Hy. (* Hy : Gamma y = Some T *) rewrite (Hagree y (by auto)) . apply Hmap. exact Hy.
```
Careful: `unfold update in Hy` gives `(if x =? y then Some T1 else Gamma y) = Some T`. Then `rewrite E in Hy` simplifies to `Gamma y = Some T` for E false. Then the subst equality lemma:
```coq
Lemma subst_open_fvar_eq : forall (sigma sigma' : atom -> tm) t x,
  (forall y, locally_closed (sigma' y)) ->

Hmm, more generally we want: if sigma' x = u, then subst sigma' (open t (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t) u. For sigma_x, u = fvar x; for sigma_u, u = u. Let's state:

```coq
Lemma subst_open_eq : forall (sigma sigma' : atom -> tm) t x,
  (forall y, locally_closed (sigma' y)) ->
  (forall y, y <> x -> sigma' y = sigma y) ->
  ~ occurs_free x t ->
  subst sigma' (open t (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t) (sigma' x).
Proof.
  intros sigma sigma' t x Hlc Hagree Hfresh.
  unfold open. rewrite subst_open_rec by exact Hlc.
  simpl. (* subst sigma' (fvar x) = sigma' x *)
  rewrite Hagree? no—need subst sigma' t = subst sigma t.
  replace (subst sigma' t) with (subst sigma t).
  - reflexivity.
  - apply subst_ext. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. intro Heq; subst; apply Hfresh; exact Hy.
Qed.
```
Wait: `rewrite subst_open_rec` gives `open_rec 0 (subst sigma' (tm_fvar x)) (subst sigma' t)`. Then `subst sigma' (tm_fvar x)` is definitionally sigma' x; simpl reduces it. And `subst sigma' t` → replace with subst sigma t ✓; result `open_rec 0 (sigma' x) (subst sigma t)` = `open (subst sigma t) (sigma' x)` ✓.

`occurs_free x t` is `In x (fvars t)`; `subst_ext` hypothesis requires `forall y, occurs_free y t -> sigma y = sigma' y`, i.e., sigma y = sigma' y. We have Hagree : y ≠ x -> sigma' y = sigma y. Given occurs_free y t and y = x would contradict Hfresh; so y ≠ x, and sigma y = sigma' y by symmetry ✓.

Now, `env_ok_update`'s hypotheses: `env_ok Gamma sigma` (Henv), `locally_closed (sigma' x)`, `LR T1 (sigma' x)`, `forall y, y <> x -> sigma' y = sigma y`.

For sigma_x:
- lc (sigma_x x) = lc (fvar x) ✓.
- LR T1 (sigma_x x) = LR T1 (fvar x) by LR_fvar ✓.
- agreement ✓.

For sigma_u0:
- lc (sigma_u0 x) = lc u0 from LR_lc ✓.
- LR T1 (sigma_u0 x) = LR T1 u0 = Hu0 ✓.
- agreement ✓.

But careful: `sigma_x x` is `(if Nat.eqb x x then tm_fvar x else sigma x)` which is definitionally reducible to `tm_fvar x` — but `Nat.eqb x x` doesn't reduce definitionally to true for variables! Nat.eqb is a Fixpoint on x; with x a variable, `Nat.eqb x x` does not reduce. So we need to rewrite with `Nat.eqb_refl x : (x =? x) = true`. So `sigma_x x` requires a rewrite: `unfold sigma_x; rewrite Nat.eqb_refl; reflexivity`. Similarly `Heq_x`'s RHS mentions `sigma_x x` which we need to rewrite.

In `env_ok_update`, the hypotheses are `locally_closed (sigma' x)` and `LR T1 (sigma' x)` — we provide these by first proving `sigma_x x = tm_fvar x` as a rewrite. Let's structure:
```
    assert (Hsigma_x_self : sigma_x x = tm_fvar x).
    { unfold sigma_x. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. }
```

```coq
(** * Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for normalization *)

(** Free-variable substitution.  Because the language is locally nameless,
    substituting for free variables never captures bound variables. *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition ids : atom -> tm := fun x => tm_fvar x.

Lemma subst_ids : forall t, subst ids t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; auto;
    try (rewrite IHt1, IHt2); try (rewrite IHt); reflexivity.
Qed.
```

  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.

Free variables:
```coq
Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Definition occurs_free (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop := In x (fvars t).
```

Fresh atoms:
```coq
Fixpoint max_atoms (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_atoms xs)
  end.

Lemma max_atoms_spec : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_atoms l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin].
    + subst. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atoms l); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh_atom (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_atoms l).

Lemma fresh_atom_spec : forall l, ~ In (fresh_atom l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh_atom in Hin.
  pose proof (max_atoms_spec l (S (max_atoms l)) Hin). lia.
Qed.
```

```coq
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x)).
```
But wait: `LR` must be defined before env_ok. Yes, define LR first.

LR:
```coq
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

Now order of lemmas matters: LR_lc, LR_sn, LR_arrow_app, LR_fvar, LR_stuck etc. Let's order:
1. subst, fvars, fresh, lc basics, open/subst commutation, update, SN basics, step/value lemmas.
2. LR definition.
3. LR_lc, LR_sn (by induction on T).
4. value lemmas: value_app_inv, value_no_step, value_SN, abs_no_step, fvar_no_step, app_fvar_no_step (maybe not needed).
5. step_lc, SN_step.
6. LR_arrow_app (projection).
7. LR_stuck (requires value_app_inv).
8. LR_fvar.
9. LR_step (requires step_lc, SN_step, LR_arrow_app... and IH at T2).
10. LR_neutral_expand (requires LR_arrow_app, LR_sn, value_app_inv, abs_no_step? no).
11. LR_abs_app (requires SN induction, LR_neutral_expand, abs_no_step, LR_step).
12. env_ok_update.
13. fundamental.
14. normalization.

Wait, LR_stuck needs LR_lc — fine. LR_neutral_expand needs LR_arrow_app. 

Let me double check the SN induction in LR_abs_app. I'll write:
```coq
Lemma LR_abs_app : forall A B (t1 : tm),
  lc_at 1 t1 ->
  (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) ->
  forall u, LR A u -> LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A t1) u).

Lemma LR_step : forall T t t', LR T t -> step t t' -> LR T t'.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' HLR Hst; simpl in *.
  - destruct HLR as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
  - destruct HLR as [Hlc [Hsn Happ]]. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + split.
      * apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
      * intros u Hu. apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).
        -- apply Happ. exact Hu.

Qed.

Now, the `LR_stuck` lemma:
```coq
Lemma LR_stuck : forall T t,
  locally_closed t -> ~ value t -> (forall t', step t t' -> False) -> LR T t.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t Hlc Hnv Hns; simpl.
  - split.
    + exact Hlc.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. exfalso. apply Hns. exact Hst.
  - split.
    + exact Hlc.
    + split.
      * apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. exfalso. apply Hns. exact Hst.

```coq
Lemma LR_neutral_expand : forall T t,
  locally_closed t -> strongly_normalizing t -> ~ value t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> LR T t') -> LR T t.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t Hlc Hsn Hnv Hred; simpl.
  - split; assumption.
  - split.
    + exact Hlc.
    + split.
      * exact Hsn.
      * intros u Hu.
        assert (Hred_app : forall t', step (tm_app t u) t' -> LR T2 t').
        { intros t' Hst.
          inversion Hst as [TA ta v HlcA HvA | t1a t1a' t2a Hstep1 Hlc2 | v1 t2a t2a' Hv1 Hstep2]; subst.
          - exfalso. apply Hnv. apply v_abs. exact HlcA.
          - apply LR_arrow_app with (T1 := T1) (t := t1a') (u := u).
            apply Hred. exact Hstep1. exact Hu.
          - exfalso. apply Hnv. exact Hv1. }
        apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).
        + apply lc_app. exact Hlc. apply LR_lc with (T := T1). exact Hu.
        + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. apply LR_sn with (T := T2). apply Hred_app. exact Hst.
        + intro Hv. exact (value_app_inv _ _ Hv).
        + exact Hred_app.
Qed.
```
Wait: in the ST_AppAbs case after inversion, the head was unified: `t = tm_abs TA ta`. Then Hnv : ~ value (tm_abs TA ta)? After subst, Hnv's type changes accordingly ✓. Apply v_abs with HlcA ✓.

Hmm, but for ST_App1: `apply Hred` gives goal `LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1a'`; Hred : forall t', step t t' -> LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t' ✓ with Hstep1 : step t t1a'. And `LR_arrow_app with (T1 := T1) (t := t1a') (u := u)` requires LR T1 u ✓ Hu.

Now `LR_abs_app`:
```coq
Lemma LR_abs_app : forall A B (t1 : tm),
  lc_at 1 t1 ->
  (forall u, LR A u -> LR B (open t1 u)) ->
  forall u, LR A u -> LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A t1) u).
Proof.
  intros A B t1 Hlc1 Hbeta.
  assert (Hgen : forall u, strongly_normalizing u ->
      forall (A : ty) (B : ty) (t1 : tm), lc_at 1 t1 ->
      (forall w, LR A w -> LR B (open t1 w)) ->
      LR A u -> LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A t1) u)).
  { intros u0 Hsn. induction Hsn as [u0 Hred IH].
    intros A0 B0 t1 Hlc1 Hbeta Hu.
    assert (Hred_app : forall t', step (tm_app (tm_abs A0 t1) u0) t' -> LR B0 t').
    { intros t' Hst.
      inversion Hst as [TA ta v HlcA HvA | x1 x1' x2 Hstep1 Hlc2 | v1 x2 x2' Hv1 Hstep2]; subst.
      - apply Hbeta. exact Hu.
      - exfalso. eapply abs_no_step. exact Hstep1.
      - apply (IH x2' Hstep2 A0 B0 t1 Hlc1 Hbeta (LR_step A0 u0 x2' Hu Hstep2)). }
    apply LR_neutral_expand with (T := B0) (t := tm_app (tm_abs A0 t1) u0).
    - apply lc_app. apply lc_abs. exact Hlc1. apply LR_lc with (T := A0). exact Hu.
    - apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. apply LR_sn with (T := B0). apply Hred_app. exact Hst.
    - intro Hv. exact (value_app_inv _ _ Hv).
    - exact Hred_app. }
  intros u Hu.
  apply Hgen with (A := A) (B := B) (t1 := t1).
  - apply LR_sn with (T := A). exact Hu.
  - exact Hlc1.
  - exact Hbeta.
  - exact Hu.
Qed.
```
Concerns:
- In Hgen's inner proof, `induction Hsn as [u0 Hred IH]`: the induction principle's case has u0, Hred, IH. But the goal mentions `u` (Hgen's first binder). Since Hsn : strongly_normalizing u and we intro'd u0 before Hsn, the `induction Hsn` will generalize u? Hmm: Hgen's statement `forall u, strongly_normalizing u -> ...`. After `intros u0 Hsn`, we have u0 and Hsn : SN u0. `induction Hsn` will look at the goal `forall A B t1, ... LR A u0 -> ...` which mentions u0. Coq's induction on Hsn will abstract u0 in the goal (since Hsn's index is u0) and produce the case with u0 renamed. This should work, giving IH : forall u', step u0 u' -> (forall A B t1, ... LR A u' -> ...). 

- In the ST_AppAbs case of Hred_app: after inversion, t1 = ta? The rule's TA ta v matched abs A0 t1: TA = A0, ta = t1, v = u0. So goal LR B0 (open t1 u0), and Hbeta u0 Hu : LR B0 (open t1 u0) ✓. `apply Hbeta; exact Hu` — but Hbeta's binder is named w in Hgen's context (`forall w, LR A0 w -> LR B0 (open t1 w)`), applying to u0 works by unification with the goal ✓.

- In ST_App1: Hstep1 : step (tm_abs A0 t1) x1'. abs_no_step A0 t1 x1' Hstep1 : False ✓.

- In ST_App2: Hstep2 : step u0 x2' ✓; IH x2' Hstep2 : forall A B t1, lc_at 1 t1 -> ... -> LR A x2' -> LR B (app (abs A t1) x2'). Then apply with A0 B0 t1 Hlc1 Hbeta and LR_step A0 u0 x2' Hu Hstep2 ✓. Now `env_ok_update`:
```coq
Lemma env_ok_update : forall Gamma x T1 (sigma sigma' : atom -> tm),
  env_ok Gamma sigma ->
  locally_closed (sigma' x) ->
  LR T1 (sigma' x) ->
  (forall y, y <> x -> sigma' y = sigma y) ->
  env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma'.
Proof.
  intros Gamma x T1 sigma sigma' Henv Hlcx HLRx Hagree.
  destruct Henv as [Hslc Hsmap]. split.
  - intros y. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. exact Hlcx.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. rewrite Hagree.
      * apply Hslc.
      * intro H; apply E. symmetry. exact H.
  - intros y T Hy. unfold update in Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy. inversion Hy. subst. exact HLRx.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. rewrite E in Hy. simpl in Hy. rewrite Hagree.
      * apply Hsmap. exact Hy.
      * intro H; apply E. symmetry. exact H.
Qed.
```
Careful: in the first branch after `subst y`, the goal is `locally_closed (sigma' x)` ✓ Hlcx.
In the second branch, E : x <> y, goal `locally_closed (sigma' y)`; rewrite Hagree (needs y <> x): `intro H; apply E; symmetry; exact H` ✓.
For the map part: Hy after `unfold update` : `(if x =? y then Some T1 else Gamma y) = Some T`. `destruct` in Hy? I did `destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E` — but Hy contains the same expression; will it be rewritten? The `destruct ... eqn:E` will abstract occurrences of `Nat.eqb x y` in the goal but NOT in Hy (a hypothesis). Need `destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E in Hy` or rewrite E in Hy. Since we did `unfold update in Hy`, Hy contains `Nat.eqb x y`; the destruct won't affect it unless we use `destruct ... eqn:E in Hy` (which also affects the goal? The `in` clause destructs in the hypothesis and goal? No—`destruct e eqn:E in H` destructs in H only, but E still records the equation). Hmm, this is getting complicated. Better approach: case split on `Nat.eqb x y` and then rewrite in Hy:
```
    + (* E : (x =? y) = true *) apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy. simpl in Hy. inversion Hy. subst. exact HLRx.
```

```
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. rewrite E in Hy. simpl in Hy. rewrite Hagree; [apply Hsmap; exact Hy | intro H; apply E; symmetry; exact H].
```
✓ where E : x <> y.

But the `destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E` in the goal part: we're in the goal `LR T (sigma' y)`. Note the goal does not contain the eqb (that was for the lc part). One more thing: in T_Abs, `IHbody` must be the IH from the induction. But actually the induction hypothesis for the T_Abs premise might be named differently. Let's write the induction with an `as` pattern:

```coq
(** ** Free-variable substitution and freshness *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition ids : atom -> tm := fun x => tm_fvar x.

Lemma subst_ids : forall t, subst ids t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Definition occurs_free (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop := In x (fvars t).

Fixpoint max_atoms (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_atoms xs)
  end.

Lemma max_atoms_spec : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_atoms l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin].
    + subst a. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atoms l); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh_atom (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_atoms l).

Lemma fresh_atom_spec : forall l, ~ In (fresh_atom l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh_atom in Hin.
  pose proof (max_atoms_spec l (S (max_atoms l)) Hin). lia.
Qed.
```
Careful in max_atoms_spec: after destruct, `subst a`? The list is `a :: l` and `In x (a :: l)`; destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin] where Heq : x = a. Then goal `x <= Nat.max a (max_atoms l)`. With `subst a`? We want to rewrite x = a or a = x. `subst a` requires a = x; Heq : x = a. Use `subst x`? Goal has x too. Let's do: `rewrite Heq. apply Nat.le_max_l.` ✓. Wait Heq : x = a, so rewriting Heq in the goal changes x to a ✓.

The second: goal `x <= Nat.max a (max_atoms l)`; apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atoms l): need x <= max_atoms l (IH with Hin) and max_atoms l <= Nat.max a (max_atoms l) (Nat.le_max_r) ✓.

```
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  apply LR_sn with (T := T).
  apply (fundamental empty t T Hty ids).

  - intros x T0 Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate.
Qed.
```
Wait: `apply (fundamental empty t T Hty ids)` leaves the goal `env_ok empty ids`, then split etc. And the goal after `apply LR_sn with (T := T)` is `LR T t`; applying fundamental gives `LR T (subst ids t)`. Is that convertible to `LR T t`? `subst ids t` is not definitionally equal to t (for an arbitrary t, subst ids (abs T t1) = abs T (subst ids t1), not definitionally abs T t1). So we need to rewrite with subst_ids:
```
  rewrite <- subst_ids. apply (fundamental empty t T Hty ids). ...
```
Hmm: goal `LR T t`; `rewrite <- subst_ids` where subst_ids : forall t, subst ids t = t. Rewriting <- in goal LR T t: the pattern `subst ids ?t` doesn't occur in the goal `LR T t`, so rewrite won't find it. We should instead do:
```
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty ids) as H.
  rewrite subst_ids in H. 
```
Wait, H : LR T (subst ids t); `rewrite subst_ids in H` → H : LR T t ✓ (subst_ids is `forall t, subst ids t = t`, so rewrites LHS to RHS). Then `exact H` after proving env_ok. So:
```
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  apply LR_sn with (T := T).
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty ids) as H.
  assert (Henv : env_ok empty ids).
  { split.
    - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T0 Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate. }
  specialize (H Henv).
  rewrite subst_ids in H.
  exact H.
Qed.
```

(** * Toolkit for the strong normalization proof. *)

(** ** Free-variable substitution *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition ids : atom -> tm := fun x => tm_fvar x.

Lemma subst_ids : forall t, subst ids t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

(** Free variables of a term, as a list. *)

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Definition occurs_free (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop := In x (fvars t).

(** A supply of fresh atoms. *)

Fixpoint max_atoms (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_atoms xs)
  end.

Lemma max_atoms_spec : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_atoms l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin].
    + rewrite Heq. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atoms l); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh_atom (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_atoms l).

Lemma fresh_atom_spec : forall l, ~ In (fresh_atom l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh_atom in Hin.
  pose proof (max_atoms_spec l (S (max_atoms l)) Hin). lia.
Qed.
```

Wait: in max_atoms_spec, `destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin]` where Hin : In x (a :: l). The `In` for cons is `x = a \/ In x l`. So Heq : x = a. `rewrite Heq` — goal `x <= Nat.max a (max_atoms l)`; rewriting x to a gives `a <= Nat.max a (max_atoms l)` ✓.

```coq
Lemma SN_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.

Qed.
```

Destructing Hsn : SN t gives t0 and Hred : forall t', step t0 t' -> SN t', plus possibly an equation t = t0. Coq's destruct for indexed inductives will try to substitute t := t0 in the goal; since the goal is SN t' (not involving t), it should be fine either way; but Hst : step t t' must match Hred's expected `step t0 t'`. If t and t0 are not unified, `apply Hred; exact Hst` fails.

To be safe, use `inversion Hsn; subst; apply Hred; exact Hst`, or:
```
  intros t t' Hsn Hst. inversion Hsn as [t0 Hred]. subst. apply Hred. exact Hst.
```

第 57 次思考 | 原始 trace 第 182 行

The final clean compile succeeded. 36 Qed's.

Summary of the solution:

**Approach: Girard–Tait unary logical relation with a semantic context interpretation.**

1. **Toolkit:**
   - Free-variable substitution `subst` (capture-free by construction in locally nameless), identity substitution lemma.
   - Free-variable lists `fvars`, `occurs_free`, and a computable fresh atom `fresh_atom` with proof of freshness.
   - Local-closure lemmas: monotonicity, opening preserves closure, opening-inversion (from `lc (open t u)` infer `lc_at 1 t`), opening of locally closed terms is inert.
   - Substitution commutation with opening: `subst σ (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ t)` for locally closed σ-images, and the key "open at a fresh variable" identity `subst σ' (open t (fvar x)) = open (subst σ t) (σ' x)`.
   - Step/value facts: values don't step, application of `fvar`/stuck terms doesn't step, step preserves local closure, SN is preserved by step.

2. **Logical relation:**
   ```
   LR Bool t      := lc t ∧ SN t
   LR (A -> B) t  := lc t ∧ SN t ∧ ∀ u, LR A u -> LR B (t u)
   ```
   Proved: `LR ⇒ lc`, `LR ⇒ SN`, closure under one-step reduction, "neutral expansion" (a locally closed non-value all of whose reducts are related is related), and the abstraction-application lemma by induction on the SN derivation of the argument, using the beta-reduct hypothesis.

3. **Context interpretation (`env_ok`)** plus the fundamental theorem:
   ```
   Γ ⊢ t : T  ⇒  ∀ σ, env_ok Γ σ  ⇒  LR T (σ t)
   ```
   The `T_Abs` case picks a fresh atom `x` (not in `L` and not free in the body), builds environments `σ_x` (mapping x to `fvar x`, for local closure) and `σ_u` (mapping x to any related argument u, for the beta-reduct property), uses the induction hypothesis on the opened body, and concludes via the abstraction-application lemma.

4. **Target theorem:** instantiate with `empty` and the identity substitution, using `env_ok empty ids` (vacuous mapping) and `subst ids t = t`, to get `LR T t`, hence `strongly_normalizing t`.

Verification:
- Fresh `coqc Task.v` compiles with exit 0 and no warnings.
- `Print Assumptions normalization` and `Print Assumptions fundamental` both report "Closed under the global context" (no axioms/admitted).
- Supplied definitions are byte-identical; the theorem statement is unchanged.
- Sanity check on `(λx:Bool. x) true` confirms the typing judgment is inhabited and the theorem applies.
